\mbox{where}\,D(\mathbb{G})=\{\phi\in\mathbb{B},\mathbb{G}\phi\in\mathbb{B}\}\end{array}\right.

(8.3)

iii) The operator
\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\mathbb{G}^{-\delta}
is bounded
\forall

\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Now, with the aid of the results of Angelscu et al
[Angelscu et al ]
or of Zagrebnov
[Zagrebnov1, Zagrebnov2 ]
with Ginibre-Gruber inequality
[Ginibre]
, it easy to prove that the series of general term
S_{k}(t)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
defined by:

S_{0}(t)\phi=e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}\phi

and

S_{k+1}(t)\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}S_{k}(t)\phi ds}

converges to
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
(nuclear norm).

By using the properties i), ii) and iii) we get:

\noindent{\small\displaystyle{\mid\mid e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid_{1}=t\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}+}\displaystyle{\mid\mid(\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G})^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid_{1}O(t^{2})};\hskip 9.24994ptt\rightarrow 0^{+}}

(8.4)

To establish the above results, we begin by given :

- In subsection 8.2, some spectral properties of semigroups
e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}
and
e^{-t(\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}+\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda})}
in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

- In subsection 8.3, we give the proof of the above formula (8.4) for the trace of
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}
.

8.2
Some spectral properties of semigroups
e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}
and
e^{-t(\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}+\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda})}
in Carleman spaces
\mathcal{C}_{p}

We begin by given some elementary spectral properties of
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
:

Lemma 8.2
.

1) The operator
\mathbb{G}
is self adjoint with compact resolvent.

2) The eigenvalues of
\mathbb{G}
are
\lambda_{n}=n(n-1)(n-2)
for
n\geq 0
associated to eigenvectors
\displaystyle{e_{n}(z)=\frac{z^{n}}{\sqrt{n!}}}
.

3)
Lim\mid\mid\mathbb{G}e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid=\frac{1}{e}
as
t\rightarrow 0.

4) the resolvent of
\mathbb{G}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall p>\frac{1}{3}
.

5)
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}
is nuclear semigroup and
\mid\mid e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid_{1}\leq Ct^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

where the constant
C
does not depend on
t

6)
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall p>0
.

proof

1) It is evident that
\mathbb{G}
is self-adjoint and its resolvent is compact follows easily from the following Rellich theorem :

Theorem 8.3
.

- (see
[Fréchet]
, p. 386)

Let
\mathbb{B}
be a self adjoint operator in a complex Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
such that

<\mathbb{B}\phi,\phi>\quad\geq\quad<\phi,\phi>,\phi\in D(\mathbb{B})
, where
D(\mathbb{B})=\{\phi\in\mathcal{H};\mathbb{B}\phi\in\mathcal{H}\}
.

Then
\mathbb{B}
is discrete if and only if
\{\phi\in D(\mathbb{B})
;
<\mathbb{B}\phi,\phi)\leq 1\}
is pre-compact.

that the operator
\mathbb{G}
is discrete.
\square

Also, we can prove the above result by using the following observation:

Since
D(A)
is compactly embedded in Bargmann space
\mathbb{B}
and
D(A^{*3}A^{3})\hookrightarrow D(A)
is continuous then
D(A^{*3}A^{3})
is compactly embedded in Bargmann space
\mathbb{B}
.

Since
(A^{*3}A^{3}+\mathbb{I})
is invertible then the operator
\mathbb{G}
is discrete.

2) It is obvious.

3) As
\mathbb{G}e^{-t\mathbb{G}}e_{n}=n(n-1)(n-2)e^{-tn(n-1)(n-2)}e_{n}
we get

\mid\mid\mathbb{G}e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid=\frac{1}{t}\quad_{{}_{n}}\!\!\!\!\!Sup\quad tn(n-1)(n-2)e^{-tn(n-1)(n-2)}=\frac{1}{e}
this implies

\noindent\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0}\mid\mid\mathbb{G}e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid=\frac{1}{e}

(8.5)

4) As
\mathbb{G}
is self adjoint and its eigenvalues are :

\lambda_{n}=n(n-1)(n-2)\sim n^{3}

(8.6)

this implies that the series of general term
\frac{1}{n^{3p}}
converges
\forall p>\frac{1}{3}
and consequently, the resolvent of
\mathbb{G}
belongs to Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall p>\frac{1}{3}
.

5)
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}
is self adjoint and it is of trace class, because the series of general term
e^{-t\lambda_{n}}
converges
\forall t>0
.

Now let
x\in[0,+\infty[
and if
t\in[0,+\infty[
, consider the function
f(x)=e^{-tx^{3}}
and its derivative
f^{\prime}(x)=-3tx^{2}e^{-tx^{3}}
which non positive then the function
f(x)
is decreasing and we have

\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-tn^{3}}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-tx^{3}}dx}

By a change of variable in the above integral, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-tx^{3}}dx=Ct^{\frac{-1}{3}}}

where the constant
C
does not depend on
t

This implies that

\mid\mid e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\mid\mid_{1}\leq\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{-t^{3}n}\leq Ct^{\frac{-1}{3}}}

6)
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}
is Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall p>0
, because the series of general term
e^{-tp\lambda_{n}}
converges
\forall t>0
and
\forall p>0
.

Remark 8.4
.

We can derive the property 6) from the fact that
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
. In fact, since
\mathcal{C}_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
then
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall p>1
.

Now if
p<1
, we choose an integer
n
such that
\frac{1}{n}<p
, then
\mathcal{C}_{\frac{1}{n}}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

Since
e^{-\frac{t}{n}\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}

\forall

\tau=\frac{t}{n}>0
then
(e^{-\frac{t}{n}\mathbb{G}})^{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\frac{1}{n}}

and we obtain that
e^{-t\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall

p>0
.

From the above remark, we are now ready to prove following lemma :

Lemma 8.5
.

1) Let
\mathbb{T}(t)
be a semigroup on a complex Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. We assume that
\mathbb{T}(t)
is selfadjoint and there exists
p_{0}>0
such that
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathbb{C}_{p_{0}}

\forall

t>0
.

Then
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall

t>0
,
\forall

p>0
.

2) Let
\mathbb{T}(t)
be a semigroup on a complex Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. We assume that there exists
p_{0}>0

such that
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p_{0}}

\forall

t>0
. Then

\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}

\forall

t>0
,
\forall

p>0
.

Proof

1) let
t=\frac{\tau}{p_{0}}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)=\mathbb{T}(\frac{\tau}{p_{0}})
then
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p_{0}}
if and only if
\hat{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
. Since
\mathcal{C}_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>1
, it follows that
\hat{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>1
.

Let
0<p<1
, Since
\hat{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
is self adjoint, then for each fixed
\tau>0
, there exist a positive
decreasing sequence
s_{n}\in l_{1}
and an orthonormal sequence
e_{n}
such that
\hat{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s_{n}e_{n}\otimes e_{n}}
.

Let
p=\frac{1}{\delta}
with
\delta>1
and
n
such that
n>\delta
, then for fixed
\frac{t}{n}
, there exist a positive decreasing sequence
r_{k}\in l_{1}
and an orthonormal sequence
e_{k}
such tat
\mathbb{T}(\frac{t}{n})=\displaystyle{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}r_{k}e_{k}\otimes e_{k}}
.

It follows that
\mathbb{T}(t)=[\mathbb{T}(\frac{t}{n})]=\displaystyle{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}r_{k}^{n}e_{k}\otimes e_{k}}
.

Since,
r_{k}\in l_{1}
then
r_{k}^{n}\in l_{\frac{1}{n}}\subset l_{p}
,i.e.
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

2) If
p>p_{0}
then
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
t>0
and all
p>p_{0}
because
\mathcal{C}_{p_{0}}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>p_{0}
.

If
p<p_{0}
, we choose an integer
n
such that
\frac{p_{0}}{n}<p
, since
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p_{0}}
for all
t>0
then
\mathbb{T}(\frac{t}{n})\in\mathcal{C}_{p_{0}}

and consequently
\mathbb{T}(t)=[\mathbb{T}(\frac{t}{n}]^{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\frac{p_{0}}{n}}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
.
\square

Remark 8.6
.

Let
\mathbb{T}
be a compact operator, we assume that
\mathbb{T}
is positif and
\mid\mid\mathbb{T}\mid\mid\leq 1
then there exist a positive sequence
0<s_{n}<1
and an orthonormal sequence
e_{n}
such that
\mathbb{T}=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s_{n}e_{n}\otimes e_{n}}
.

Let
\mathbb{T}(t)=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s_{n}^{t}e_{n}\otimes e_{n}}
, if we choose
\displaystyle{s_{n}\in\bigcap_{p=0}^{\infty}l_{p}}
where
l_{p}
is the space of
p
-summable sequences, then
\mathbb{T}(t)\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
t>0
and all
p>0
, it follows that
\mid\mid\mathbb{T}(t)\mid\mid_{p}=\displaystyle{(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}s_{n}^{tp})^{\frac{1}{p}}}

For
s<t
we have
s_{n}^{t}<s_{n}^{s}
then
\mid\mid\mathbb{T}(t)\mid\mid_{p}<\mid\mid\mathbb{T}(s)\mid\mid_{p}
.

By using the Beppo-Levi’s theorem, we deduce that
\mid\mid\mathbb{T}(t)\mid\mid_{p}\rightarrow\infty
as
t\rightarrow 0
.

Proposition 8.7
.

Let
\mathbb{B}_{0}=\{\phi\in\mathbb{B};\phi(0)=0\}
,
\mathbb{P}_{0}=\{p\in\mathbb{P};p(0)=0\}
where
\mathbb{P}
is the space of polynomials

and

\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}
with domain
D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})
is the closure of the restriction of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
on
\mathbb{P}_{0}
.

Then we have:

(a)

\forall

\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that :

\mid\mid\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi\mid\mid\leq\epsilon\mid\mid\mathbb{G}\phi\mid\mid+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid\,\forall\,\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})

(8.7)

(b)

\forall

\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that :

\mid<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>\mid\leq\epsilon<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}\,\forall\,\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})

(8.8)

(c)
For
\lambda^{\prime\prime}>0
and
\forall

\epsilon
;
0<\epsilon<\lambda^{\prime\prime}
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that :

\Re e<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>\geq(\lambda^{\prime\prime}-\epsilon)<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>-C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}\,\forall\,\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})

(8.9)

in particular, the range of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
is closed.

(d)
For
\lambda^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\geq 0
and
\mu>0
,
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
is accretive and maximal.

(e)

D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})=D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})=D(\mathbb{G})
.

(f)

-\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
generates an analytic semigroup
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}}
,
t>0
.

(g)
For
\lambda^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0
, the resolvent of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
belongs to Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>\frac{1}{3}
.

Proof

a) Let
\phi\in\mathbb{B}_{0}
, we have
\phi(z)=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}e_{n}(z)}
. Then

\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi(z)=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}[\mu na_{n}+i\lambda(n-1)\sqrt{n}a_{n-1}+i\lambda n\sqrt{n+1}a_{n+1}]e_{n}(z)}

and

\mathbb{G}\phi(z)=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n(n-1)(n-2)a_{n}e_{n}(z)}

we remark that there exists
C>0
such that
\mid\mid\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi\mid\mid^{2}\leq C\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{3}\mid a_{n}\mid^{2}}

and

\mid\mid\mathbb{G}\phi\mid\mid^{2}\geq\frac{1}{36}\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{6}\mid a_{n}\mid^{2}}
.

Now, by using the Young’s inequality, we get:

\forall\epsilon>0
,
k^{3}\leq\epsilon k^{6}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}

\forall k\in\mathbb{N}
.

this implies that :

\forall

\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that
\mid\mid\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi\mid\mid\leq\epsilon\mid\mid\mathbb{G}\phi\mid\mid+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid
for all
\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})
.

b)
<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>=\mu\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid^{2}+i\lambda<A^{2}\phi,A\phi>+i\lambda<A\phi,A^{2}\phi>
for all
\phi\in D(\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda})
.

Then

\mid<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>\mid\leq\mu\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid^{2}+2\mid\lambda\mid\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid.\mid\mid A^{2}\phi\mid\mid
pour tout
\phi\in D(\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda})
.

With the aid of following inequalities:

i)
\forall

\epsilon_{1}>0

\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid.\mid\mid A^{2}\phi\mid\mid\leq\epsilon_{1}\mid\mid A^{2}\phi\mid\mid^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid^{2}.

ii)
\forall

\epsilon_{2}>0
there exists
C_{\epsilon_{2}}>0
such that
\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid^{2}\leq\epsilon_{2}\mid\mid A^{3}\phi\mid\mid^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{2}}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}

iii)
\mid\mid A^{2}\phi\mid\mid^{2}\leq\mid\mid A^{3}\phi\mid\mid^{2}

we obtain:

\forall

\epsilon>0
, there
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that
\mid<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>\mid\leq\epsilon<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}
for all
\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})
.

c) Since
Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>=\lambda^{\prime\prime}<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>+<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>
and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}>0

Then

Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>\geq\lambda^{\prime\prime}<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>-\mid<\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}\phi,\phi>\mid
.

By using the above property we get:

Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>\geq\lambda^{\prime\prime}<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>-\epsilon<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>-C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}-\epsilon)<\mathbb{G}\phi,\phi>-C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}

We choose
0<\epsilon<\lambda^{\prime\prime}
to deduce that

Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>\geq-C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}

Consequently the range of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
is closed.

d) Since
A^{*}(A+A^{*})A
is symmetric operator then

Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>=\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mid\mid a^{3}\phi\mid\mid^{2}+\mu\mid\mid a\phi\mid\mid^{2}\geq\mu\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}
,
\forall\phi\in D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})

Now for
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
and
\mu>0
we deduce that :

Re<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>\geq\mu\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}
,
\forall\phi\in D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})
.

This inequality will be not verified if we kept constant functions in Bargmann space
\mathbb{B}
.

Now, we would like to show that there exists
\beta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
;
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I}
is invertible.

We rewrite
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
in the following form:

\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}=\lambda^{\prime\prime}(\mathbb{G}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}
et
\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}=[\mathbb{I}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}](\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})
.

Using the property a) to get:

\mid\mid\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid\leq\epsilon\mid\mid\mathbb{G}(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid

\leq\epsilon\mid\mid(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I}-\beta\mathbb{I})(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid+C_{\epsilon}\mid\mid(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid

\leq\epsilon\mid\mid\psi\mid\mid+(\epsilon\beta+C_{\epsilon})\mid\mid(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid

and as
\mid\mid(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\mid\mid\leq\frac{1}{\beta}
then

\mid\mid\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\psi\mid\mid\leq(2\epsilon+\frac{C_{\epsilon}}{\beta})
.

Now, we choose
0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
and
\beta>\frac{C_{\epsilon}}{1-2\epsilon}
to obtain :

\mid\mid\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\mathbb{H}_{\mu,\lambda}(\mathbb{G}+\beta\mathbb{I})^{-1}\mid\mid<1
.

this implies
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}+\beta\mathbb{I}
is invertible.

e) We begin to show that
D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})=D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
.

First,
D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})\subset D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
is trivial.

To show that
D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})\subset D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
,
\phi\in D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
then
(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}+\beta\mathbb{I})\phi\in\mathbb{E}_{0}
for all
\beta
.

Since there exists
\beta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I}
is invertible of
D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
on
\mathbb{E}_{0}
, then there exists

\phi_{1}\in D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
such that :

(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I})\phi=(H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I})\phi_{1}
, in particular, we have
(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I})(\phi-\phi_{1})=0
.

To deduce that
\phi=\phi_{1}
, we need to verify that
Ker(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I})=\{0\}
, where
Ker
denote the kernel of the operator
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I}

we recall that the range of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I}
is closed and the formal adjoint of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
is
\mathbb{\mathbb{H}}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
where we substitute
\lambda
by its opposite.

Now, since the adjoint of the minimal is formal adjoint of the maximal and
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{min}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I}
is invertible then
Ker(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}+\beta_{0}\mathbb{I})=\{0\}
this implies that
\phi=\phi_{1}
and
\phi\in D_{min}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})
.

From the inequality a) and the theorem 111 in book’s Kato
[Kato]
, we deduce
D_{max}(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})=D(\mathbb{G})
.

f) From the inequality a) and the theorem 2.1 in book’s Pazy
[Pazy]
, we deduce that
-\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
generates analytic semigroup
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}

t>0
.

g) For
\lambda^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0
the resolvent of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
is Carleman class
\mathcal{}C_{p}
for all
p>\frac{1}{3}
, this property is the lemma 4.1 of
[Aimar6 et al]
. This ends the proof of this proposition.

Now, the above properties d) and e) allow us to show the following theorem:

Theorem 8.8
.

-\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
generates a semigroup
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
of Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>0
and all
t>0
.

Proof

Let
\mathbb{B}_{0}=\{\phi\in\mathbb{B};\phi(0)=0\}
then on
\mathbb{B}_{0}
we have:

\Re e<\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\phi,\phi>=\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}\mid\mid A^{3}\phi\mid\mid^{2}+\mu\mid\mid A\phi\mid\mid^{2}\geq\mu\mid\mid\phi\mid\mid^{2}

for
\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}\geq 0
and
\mu>0
.

From this inequality we deduce that
0
belongs to resolvent set
\rho(\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}})
of the operator
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
.

Let
\mathbb{T}(t)=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}}\phi ds
then

\mathbb{T}(t)=\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(\mathbb{I}-e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}})
and as the resolvante of
\mathbb{H}_{\lambda{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
is Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>\frac{1}{3}
and the operator
\mathbb{I}-e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
is bounded then
\mathbb{T}(t)
is Carleman class
\mathcal{C}_{p}
for all
p>\frac{1}{3}
and with the aid of lemma 8.5, we end the proof.
\square

8.3
Asymptotic expansion of trace of
e^{-t\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}

We put
S(s)=e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}}e^{-s\mathbb{H}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
then for
\phi\in D(\mathbb{G})
, the application
\phi\rightarrow S(s)\phi
is differentiable and
S^{\prime}(s)\phi=\lambda^{\prime\prime}\mathbb{G}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}-e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}=-e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
.

On
[0,t]
, we have
\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}S^{\prime}(s)\phi ds=S(t)\phi-S(0)\phi=}-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds}

This implies that
e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi
is solution of the following integral equation :

e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds}

(8.10)

or

e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}N(t,s)e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds}\mbox{ with}\,N(t,s)=e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}

(8.11)

It is well known that the solution of equation (8.10) can be obtained by successive approximation method:

e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}=\displaystyle{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}S_{k}(t)}

(8.12)

where

S_{0}(t)\phi=e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi

and

S_{k+1}(t)\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}S_{k}(t)\phi ds}

the convergence of (8.12) is in operator norm.

Notice that:

S_{1}(t)\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-t_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-t_{1}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi dt_{1}}

S_{2}(t)\phi=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{1}}e^{-(t-t_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(t_{1}-t_{2})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-t_{2}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi dt_{2}dt_{1}}}

S_{3}(t)\phi=(-1)^{3}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{-(t-t_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(t_{1}-t_{2})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(t_{2}-t_{3})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-t_{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi dt_{3}dt_{2}dt_{1}}}}

S_{k}(t)\phi=(-1)^{k}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{2}}.................................}}}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t_{k-1}}}

e^{-(t-t_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(t_{1}-t_{2})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(t_{2}-t_{3})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}............................................

e^{-(t_{k-1}-t_{k})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-t_{k}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi dt_{k}............................................dt_{3}dt_{2}dt_{1}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

The series
e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}=\displaystyle{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}S_{k}(t)}
converges in trace norm.

Proof

The convergence in trace norm is obtained by using the results of Angelescu-Nenciu-Bundaru, in particular their proposition in
[Angelescu]
or the results of Zagrebnov in
[Zagrebnov1, Zagrebnov2]
, in particular the theorem 2.1 in
[Zagrebnov2]
with the aid of Ginibre-Gruber’s inequality in
[Ginibre et al]
.
\square

Now we are going to derive an asymptotic expansion of the trace of
e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}
as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}

For
s\geq 0
we have

e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi=e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{s}N(s,s_{1})e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds_{1}}
with
N(s,s_{1})=e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}

and for
0\leq s\leq t
, we substitute the above expression of
e^{-sH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi
in (8.11) to get :

e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}N(t,s)[e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{s}N(s,s_{1})e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi]ds_{1}ds}}

=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}N(t,s)e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi ds}
+
\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{s}N(t,s)N(s,s_{1})e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds_{1}ds}}

=-\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi ds}
+
\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{s}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds_{1}ds}}

Then we have:

e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\phi ds}
+

\displaystyle{\int_{\Delta}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}\phi ds_{1}ds}

(8.13)

where
\Delta
is the triangle
\{(s_{1},s);0\leq s_{1}\leq s\leq t\}
.

The matrix associated to
H_{\mu,\lambda}
in the basis
\{e_{n}\}
can be written in this form :

H_{\mu,\lambda}e_{n}=i\lambda(n-1)\sqrt{n}e_{n-1}+n\mu e_{n}+i\lambda n\sqrt{n+1}e_{n+1}

(8.14)

- The family of infinite matrices associated to
H_{\mu,\lambda}
is tridiagonal of the form
J+i\lambda H^{(i)}
, where the matrix
J
is diagonal with entries
J_{nn}:=n\mu
, and the matrix
H^{(i)}
is off-diagonal, with nonzero entries
H_{n,n+1}^{(i)}=H_{n+1,n}^{(i)}:=H_{n}^{(i)}=n\sqrt{n+1}
.

- The family of infinite matrices associated to
\lambda^{\prime\prime}G
is diagonal with entries
G_{nn}:=\lambda^{\prime\prime}n(n-1)(n-2)

Let the infinite matrix
{}^{t\!}H_{\mu,\lambda}
be obtained from
H_{\mu,\lambda}
by transposing of the elements and the infinite matrix

H_{\mu,\lambda}^{\bot}
be obtained from
H_{\mu,\lambda}
by transposing and by taking complex conjugates of the elements. Then observe that

i)
H_{\mu,\lambda}
is symmetric complex matrix i.e.
H_{\mu,\lambda}=\,^{t\!}H_{\mu,\lambda}
.

ii)
H_{\mu,\lambda}\neq H_{\mu,\lambda}^{\bot}
(The symbol
\bot
represents Dirac Hermitian conjugation; that is, transpose and complex conjugate.)

iii) As
H_{n}^{(i)}=O(n^{\alpha})
with
\alpha=\frac{3}{2}>1
then the standard perturbation theory is not applicable.

iv) As
G_{nn}=\lambda^{\prime\prime}n(n-1)(n-2)
then
G_{nn}=O(n^{3})
as
n\rightarrow\infty

v) For other properties on the matrix associated to
H_{\mu,\lambda}
, we can consult the
[Intissar 20]
.

vi) from the above observations, we deduce that the operator
H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}
is bounded for all
\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

Now, we present the aim result of this work in following theorem:

Theorem 8.10
.

Let
H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}=\lambda^{\prime\prime}G+H_{\mu,\lambda}
the Gribov’s operator acting on Bargmann’s space.

where

G=A^{*3}A^{3}
and
H_{\mu,\lambda}=\mu A^{*}A+i\lambda A^{*}(A+A^{*})A

[A,A^{*}]=I
and
(\lambda^{\prime\prime},\mu,\lambda)
are reel parameters and
i^{2}=-1
.

Then

\mid\mid e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}=t\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}+\mid\mid(\lambda^{\prime\prime}G)^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}O(t^{2})
.

Proof

(a)
We begin by computing the trace of the operator:

I_{1}(t)=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}ds}

We have

\mid\mid I_{1}(t)\mid\mid_{1}=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\mid\mid e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}ds}

=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}<e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}e_{n},e_{n}>ds}}

=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}<e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s\lambda_{n}}e_{n},e^{s\lambda_{n}}e_{n}>ds}}
because
e^{s\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}
is self adjoint

=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}<e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e_{n},e_{n}>ds}}

=\displaystyle{\int_{0}^{t}\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}ds}

=t\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}

Then we deduce that:

\mid\mid I_{1}(t)\mid\mid_{1}=t\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}

(8.15)

(b)
We begin to recall the symmetry property of the norm in Carleman class
C_{p}

The symmetry of the norm in
C_{p}
means that

\mid\mid K_{1}K_{2}K_{3}\mid\mid_{p}\leq\mid\mid K_{1}\mid\mid.\mid\mid K_{2}\mid\mid_{p}\mid\mid K_{3}\mid\mid.

(8.16)

for any bounded operators
K_{1}
and
K_{3}
and
K_{2}\in C_{p}

Consider the trace of the operator

I_{2}(t)=\displaystyle{\int_{\Delta}e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}ds_{1}ds}

and let
\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}
such that
H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}
bounded, then

\mid\mid I_{2}(t)\mid\mid_{1}=

\displaystyle{\int_{\Delta}\mid\mid e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}[G^{\delta}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}]H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}[G^{\delta}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}]\mid\mid_{1}ds_{1}ds}

As
t
can be written as sum of three positif numbers
t=(t-s)+(s-s_{1})+s_{1}
. It follows that at least one of them is not less than
\frac{t}{3}
; suppose, for example, that
s-s_{1}\geq\frac{t}{3}

Then

\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}\leq\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}

By using the inequality (8.16) we deduce that

\mid\mid e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}[G^{\delta}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}]H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}[G^{\delta}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}]\mid\mid_{1}

\leq\mid\mid e^{-(t-s)\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}\mid\mid.\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-(s-s_{1})\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}.\mid\mid H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}[G^{\delta}e^{-s_{1}H_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}]\mid\mid

\leq\mid\mid H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}\mid\mid^{2}\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}

Then we have

\mid\mid I_{2}(t)\mid\mid_{1}\leq\mid\mid H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}\mid\mid^{2}\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}\displaystyle{\int_{\Delta}dsds_{1}}

\leq\mid\mid H_{\mu,\lambda}G^{-\delta}\mid\mid^{2}\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}t^{2}

It follows that

\mid\mid I_{2}(t)\mid\mid_{1}=\mid\mid G^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}O(t^{2})

(8.17)

and consequently we have

\mid\mid e^{-tH_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}}-e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}=t\mid\mid e^{-t\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}H_{\mu,\lambda}\mid\mid_{1}+\mid\mid(\lambda^{\prime\prime}G)^{\delta}e^{-\frac{t}{3}\lambda^{\prime\prime}G}\mid\mid_{1}O(t^{2}).

(8.18)
